  - **Starlight chrome** — pagination prev/next now mirror the link-card treatment (hover lift + glow), tables get a card container + uppercase header (inline code in headers kept case-exact) and later a readability pass (14px top-aligned body cells, zebra striping so the eye tracks across wide rows), blockquotes an accent rail + tint, the search trigger brand colors, and the sidebar gains uppercase group-label eyebrows + a real hover state + a rounded current-item (the old group-label override was a dead selector matching 0 elements). The never-wired `data-section` accent system was removed (it always resolved to teal anyway).

- **The landing hero is the live demo: real GitHub-activity data through `@wavehouse/sdk` in the visitor's browser, replacing the static terminal mock + wave-mesh animation** (`docs/src/components/LiveDemo.astro` (new), `docs/src/components/Hero.astro`, `docs/src/content/docs/index.mdx`, `docs/src/styles/global.css`, `docs/scripts/screenshot.mjs`, `docs/package.json`, `Makefile`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`): the hero's visual column now renders a live panel fed by the public read-only role on [stats.wavehouse.dev](https://stats.wavehouse.dev) (Wave-RF/WaveHouse-Stats, our own-WaveHouse dogfood deployment): every headline number from **one cached `gh_summary` pipe call** (Wave-RF/WaveHouse-Stats#19 — it also carries reconciler-observed `open_issues`/`open_prs`, live for any future `[data-wh-live]` placement), the activity feed backfilled from the **server-curated, server-deduped, cached `gh_activity_recent` pipe** (Wave-RF/WaveHouse-Stats#18 — its `(event_type, action)` pair list and the component's verb map must stay in sync; both sides carry the note), and a live SSE tail via `stream()`. The SSE leg streams all `gh_events` rows and is filtered in-browser (type-only, because live rows are raw producer payloads where an `action` IN-filter could drop rows whose `action` is absent; the verb allowlist in `addRow` is the final arbiter), a composite-key dedup absorbs webhook redeliveries and the backfill/stream overlap, and zone-less producer timestamps on live rows are normalized to UTC before parsing (backfill rows come back RFC3339; the raw SSE payload is ClickHouse-style and would otherwise read as local time). The pre-pipe reads — per-number pipes and a structured-query backfill (`168h`) — remain as runtime fallbacks, so a stats-stack rollback degrades the panel to slower, not blank. A star CTA ("Star Wave-RF/WaveHouse — and watch it land here, live") and a full-demo link close the panel, and the closer band gains a live stargazer chip fed by the same fetch (`[data-wh-live]` targets, hidden until data arrives). The panel server-renders a layout-stable skeleton (fixed-height feed, "—" counts) so the page is complete without JS and degrades to placeholders + an "offline" pill if the demo instance is unreachable; the script re-initializes per Astro view-transition mount and tears down (SSE `close()`, timers) when navigating away; styles are `is:global` because the feed rows are runtime-created DOM that Astro's scoped selectors would skip. It is fluid down to ~320px: the hero's single-column mobile track, the `.wh-hero__visual` flex wrapper, and the 3-up stat grid all carry `min-width: 0` / `minmax(0, …)` so the panel shrinks to the viewport instead of being floored at the stat grid's ~325px min-content and clipped by the hero's `overflow: hidden` (the feed-row title ellipsis-truncates, stat labels stay single-line). `@wavehouse/sdk` becomes a docs **workspace dependency** — the docs site is now a real SDK consumer — with `check-docs` and `dev-docs` gaining a `build-ts` prerequisite (astro check and the browser bundle resolve `clients/ts/dist/`). `docs/scripts/screenshot.mjs` switches `networkidle` → `load` (the SSE stream never goes idle) and waits on the visual column's entrance `animationend` instead of the deleted terminal lines, deliberately independent of live data so screenshots can't hang on the demo instance. Follow-ups: Wave-RF/WaveHouse-Stats#18–21 (curated pipe, summary pipe, embed capacity, stats-page polish), #286 (query playground page + live SDK examples in docs).

- **`make dev-docs` is now production-faithful: rebuild-on-save through the real Worker instead of `astro dev`** (`docs/scripts/dev.mjs` (new), `docs/package.json`, `docs/astro.config.mjs`, `Makefile`, `AGENTS.md`, `.gitignore`, `.vscode/launch.json`, `docs/scripts/screenshot.mjs`, `docs/src/content/docs/development.md`): the Astro dev server skips everything the Cloudflare Worker adds in production — `cloudflare-md-router` content negotiation (`.md` twins), the pagefind search index, and the `starlight-llm-tools` outputs only exist in real builds — so the de-facto docs workflow had become a manual `make build-docs && make preview-docs` cycle, restarted by hand per change. `pnpm run dev` (and therefore `make dev-docs`) now drives `docs/scripts/dev.mjs`: a debounced full `astro build` on every save to `src/`, `public/`, or a root build input (`astro.config.mjs`, `tsconfig.json`, `package.json`, `.env*` — but not `worker/index.ts`/`wrangler.jsonc`, which `wrangler dev` hot-reloads itself, and not `pnpm-lock.yaml`, which changes on `git pull` before `pnpm install` has run), with saves landing mid-build coalescing into one follow-up build, staged into a gitignored `docs/.dev-dist/` and synced in-place into `dist/` only on success (plain node fs, prune-then-copy — no rsync or any other external tool, so minimal WSL/container setups work) — a failed build prints a red banner + terminal bell and keeps serving the last good site rather than the emptied `outDir` a direct in-place build would leave — while `wrangler dev --live-reload` on :4321 (`DOCS_PORT` overrides; same default as before, so `docs/scripts/screenshot.mjs` and muscle memory still work; one-off `preview-docs` stays on :8787) picks up the asset changes live, new routes included, and refreshes the browser over its websocket. Each rebuild is a real production build with one deliberate exception: the loop sets `WAVEHOUSE_DOCS_WATCH=1`, under which `astro.config.mjs` drops `starlight-links-validator` — mid-edit prose always has dangling links, and failing every rebuild over them would mean never seeing the change being made; rendered output is identical, CI / `make build-docs` still enforce link validity, and `DOCS_WATCH_STRICT=1` keeps the validator on in watch builds for those who want broken links to fail loudly at edit time (the development.md tooling notes now spell out that split; build errors themselves — broken Mermaid, bad frontmatter, MDX errors — always fail the watch build loudly either way). The raw HMR dev server remains available as `pnpm --filter wavehouse-docs run start` for fidelity-insensitive iteration (e.g. CSS tweaking) — the VS Code launch config gains a second entry for it, with the existing `pnpm dev` entry relabeled to match its new behavior.

- **Docs Mermaid diagrams render through a per-diagram disk cache** (`docs/astro.config.mjs`, `docs/package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`; the cache itself ships in `astro-themed-mermaid` v0.2.0): diagram SSR runs headless Chromium via `rehype-mermaid` and re-rendered all 17 diagrams on *every* docs build — including builds that touched no diagram, i.e. almost all of them. The config now uses the package's new `mermaid.rehypeMermaid` export, which wraps `rehype-mermaid` with a content-addressed cache under `node_modules/.cache/astro-themed-mermaid/` (keyed on diagram source + render options + package versions, so theme and toolchain changes self-invalidate; entries are re-id'd to content-derived `mermaid-c<hash>` ids so cross-build entries can't collide on a page). A build whose diagrams all hit skips Chromium — and even the `rehype-mermaid`/playwright import — entirely: measured warm rebuilds drop from ~6–7s to ~3.7s, a single-diagram edit re-renders only its page (~5s), and output was verified byte-identical modulo SVG ids. Cold builds (fresh clone, CI, `make clean-tools`) render once and harvest; cache I/O is best-effort and degrades to a normal render, never a failed build.

- **Docs-site analytics rebuilt: PostHog routed through the first-party relay, with CI-safe config and soft-nav-correct capture** (`docs/src/components/PostHog.astro` (new), `docs/src/components/HomepageCtaTracking.astro` (new), `docs/src/components/{Head,Hero,Footer}.astro`, `docs/src/content/docs/index.mdx`, `docs/astro.config.mjs`, `docs/.gitignore`, `docs/posthog-setup-report.md` (new), `.claude/skills/integration-astro-view-transitions/` (new, vendored), `scripts/docs-prose.sh`, `docs/src/content/docs/claude-code.md`): the hardcoded PostHog head snippet in `astro.config.mjs` (pointed straight at `us.i.posthog.com`) is replaced by a `PostHog.astro` component in the Starlight `Head` override, initialized behind a `window.__posthog_initialized` guard with `capture_pageview: 'history_change'` so ClientRouter soft navigations both survive re-init and capture pageviews (verified headless: `$pageview` fires with `navigation_type: pushState`). Analytics now route through the managed first-party reverse proxy `t.wave-rf.com` — the shared `*.posthog.com` ingest domains sit on ad-blocker lists — with `ui_host` keeping toolbar/app links on `us.posthog.com`. Configuration reads `PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN` / `PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST` (gitignored `docs/.env`) and falls back to committed values: CI builds the site with no PostHog env, so env-only wiring shipped a dead snippet to production — the committed `phc_` token is the public client token that ships in every served page by design, not a secret. Three click events are instrumented — `hero_cta_clicked` (`Hero.astro`), `footer_link_clicked` (`Footer.astro`), `homepage_cta_clicked` (`HomepageCtaTracking.astro`, extracted to a component because MDX parses `{` as JSX expression syntax and a raw `<script is:inline>` body fails `astro build`) — all idempotent across hard loads and `astro:page-load` re-binds. Local-dev noise (localhost was ~85% of recent pageviews) is filtered PostHog-side via project test-account filters on `$host`, not client guard code. The wizard's vendored agent skill lands at `.claude/skills/integration-astro-view-transitions/` (inventoried in claude-code.md) and its frozen setup report at `docs/posthog-setup-report.md`, denylisted from the docs-prose review set as an internal artifact.

- **Reviewer assignment is now fully GitHub-native — removed `housekeeping.yml`'s reviewer-request, the `assign-and-request-review` composite, and the `ADMINS` list** (`.github/workflows/housekeeping.yml`, `.github/actions/assign-and-request-review/` (deleted), `.github/board-config.env`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/development.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/claude-code.md`): the `required_reviewers` ruleset rule requests the `@Wave-RF/wavehouse-admins` team on every PR, and the team's **code-review assignment** (configured on the team) auto-assigns + load-balances a specific member — so the workflow no longer picks/requests a reviewer or sets an assignee. `housekeeping.yml` is now just path-labeling + PR-title-lint. With its last consumer gone, `ADMINS` is dropped from `board-config.env` (which now only carries Task Board IDs for `triage.yml`), removing the team-membership-vs-`ADMINS` drift.

- **Retired the custom `Admin approval` status-check workflow for the native `required_reviewers` ruleset rule; de-automated Dependabot** (`.github/workflows/admin-approval.yml` (deleted), `.github/workflows/dependabot-automerge.yml` (deleted), `.github/workflows/housekeeping.yml`, `.github/board-config.env`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/development.md`; `main branch protection` ruleset): the admin-review gate is now the ruleset's `required_reviewers` rule — an approval from the `@Wave-RF/wavehouse-admins` team — replacing the 227-line `admin-approval.yml` that drove a commit-status gate (which produced a per-commit red-X / yellow-dot on every PR until approval, and kept `ADMINS` inlined in the workflow *and* in `board-config.env`). The admin bypass mode is now `pull_request` (no direct pushes to `main` for anyone; admins still self- / emergency-merge their own PR), `require_last_push_approval` is on, and a separate tag ruleset restricts `v*` / `sdk-v*` creation to admins. `dependabot-automerge.yml` (auto-approve + auto-merge of patch/minor bumps) is removed — Dependabot PRs now get a human admin review like any PR. Required status checks drop from three to two (`CI` + `PR housekeeping`).

- **Pre-push review gate is now reviewer-count-agnostic, driven by a single source of truth** (`scripts/pre-push-reviewers.sh` (new), `.claude/hooks/agent-bash-gate.sh`, `.claude/hooks/review-marker.sh`, `.claude/commands/prepush.md`, `.claude/commands/docs-review.md`, `.claude/agents/pre-push-reviewer.md`, `.claude/agents/docs-reviewer.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/claude-code.md`): the two mandatory reviewers (`pre-push-reviewer`, `docs-reviewer`) were hardcoded in both hooks, `/prepush`, AGENTS.md, and the docs, so adding a third (e.g. a security reviewer) meant editing all of them. Now `scripts/pre-push-reviewers.sh` lists the gating subagents — one name per line — and is the *only* place the set is defined: `agent-bash-gate.sh` requires a marker for every listed reviewer (fail-closed if the list is missing, empty, or corrupt), `review-marker.sh` writes the marker for whichever listed reviewer returns `VERDICT: ship_it`, and `/prepush` reads the list to launch the relevant reviewers in parallel. Marker names are now **derived from the reviewer name** — `tmp/<name>-passed-<HEAD-sha>` (`tmp/review-passed-*` → `tmp/pre-push-reviewer-passed-*`, `tmp/docs-review-passed-*` → `tmp/docs-reviewer-passed-*`); markers live in gitignored `tmp/`, so the rename needs no migration. Adding a reviewer is now two steps with **no** hook edits — create `.claude/agents/<name>.md`, then add `<name>` to the list (AGENTS.md §"Adding a pre-push reviewer").

- **Pre-push reviewers run only where relevant — trivial changes skip the rest, on the record** (`scripts/skip-pre-push-review.sh` (new), `.claude/commands/prepush.md`, `.claude/skills/pr-review-locally/SKILL.md`, `.claude/hooks/agent-bash-gate.sh`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/claude-code.md`): `/prepush` now judges which reviewers a change actually needs (the code reviewer is pointless on a docs-only typo; the docs reviewer on a test-only change) and runs those in parallel, **skipping** the rest via `scripts/skip-pre-push-review.sh <name> "<reason>"` — a sanctioned, logged skip that writes the reviewer's marker (so the push proceeds) and appends the reason to `tmp/review-skips-<HEAD>.log`. The skip command refuses unknown reviewer names and prints a ⚠️ when a skip looks wrong (skipping docs review while docs files changed, or code review while non-docs files changed); `agent-bash-gate.sh` echoes the skip log at push time so skips are auditable. This deliberately trades a little safety for speed — the orchestrator self-certifies skips — so the rule is *bias to running; when unsure, run it (or `/prepush all` to force the full set)*. Separately, the `pr-review-locally` skill (auditing someone else's PR) now runs all the **relevant** reviewers in parallel, not just the code reviewer, surfacing their combined findings.

- **PR titles are validated by one shared rule — locally before `gh pr create` and in the required `PR housekeeping` check** (`scripts/lint-pr-title.sh` (new), `.github/workflows/housekeeping.yml`, `.claude/hooks/agent-bash-gate.sh`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/development.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/claude-code.md`): the Conventional-Commits title rule (12 types, lowercase-first subject, no trailing period, ≤ 72 chars — the title becomes the squash-merge subject on `main`) previously lived only as a regex inside `housekeeping.yml`, so a malformed title was discovered after the PR existed, when the required check failed — the recurring "title too long / wrong format" round-trip. The rule now lives in `scripts/lint-pr-title.sh` (single source of truth): `housekeeping.yml` calls it for the required check, `agent-bash-gate.sh` lints quoted `--title`/`-t` values on `gh pr create` / `gh pr edit --title` so agents catch a bad title before the PR exists (fail-open when no quoted title is parseable), and humans can validate with `scripts/lint-pr-title.sh "<title>"`. `PR_TITLE_SKIP_LENGTH=1` exempts Dependabot's long grouped-update titles from the length cap; `PR_TITLE_MAX_LEN` overrides it.

- **AGENTS.md gains craftsmanship + review-cooperation rules** (`AGENTS.md`): a new §Craftsmanship codifies three cross-cutting habits for every change — comment the *why* not the *what* (concise, matching the file's density), DRY / one-source-of-truth, and leave-it-neater-than-you-found-it (small safe cleanups in passing; large or uncertain ones become a tracking issue). §Review Response now requires *addressing* every reviewer finding — fixing small valid out-of-scope ones cooperatively, opening (or linking an existing) tracking issue for ones too large or uncertain to do in-PR, and never silently dropping a flag — and Operating Rules #2/#4 are reworded to match (the push needs *every* reviewer, not "both"; address-and-resolve, not just resolve).

- **`docs-reviewer` is now a mandatory, always-on pre-push gate, run in parallel with `pre-push-reviewer`** (`scripts/docs-prose.sh` (new), `.claude/hooks/review-marker.sh`, `.claude/hooks/agent-bash-gate.sh`, `.claude/agents/docs-reviewer.md`, `.claude/agents/pre-push-reviewer.md`, `.github/prompts/docs-review.md`, `.github/prompts/pr-review.md`, `.claude/commands/docs-review.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/claude-code.md`): the docs-prose review was previously advisory (no marker, soft-nudged by a `pre-push-reviewer` `[SHOULD]`). It now emits a `VERDICT: ship_it|iterate|block` line in its default (branch) scope; the `review-marker.sh` SubagentStop hook writes `tmp/docs-reviewer-passed-<HEAD-sha>` on `ship_it`, and `agent-bash-gate.sh` requires **both** that marker and the existing `tmp/pre-push-reviewer-passed-<HEAD-sha>` before any push to a PR (non-main) branch with commits ahead of `main` — unconditionally, even for code-only changes, because the reviewer also checks **code↔docs sync** (code that changed but whose docs didn't, per §Documentation Sync). Scope widened from "Starlight site + top-level `*.md`" to a denylist resolved by `scripts/docs-prose.sh`: every tracked `.md`/`.mdx` except `.claude/**`, `.github/**`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `*.draft.md`/`*.old.md`, `PERF-CLAIMS-REVIEW.md` — so the README (incl. the SDK's `clients/ts/README.md`), `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, and `SUPPORT.md` are now covered and new docs are picked up automatically (`CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`/`SUPPORT.md` deep-reviewed only on change). An explicit `/docs-review <path>` or `/docs-review all` stays advisory (no `VERDICT:`, no marker). The `pre-push-reviewer` docs-prose soft-gate is removed (the hard gate replaces it); the soft-gate nudge in `.github/prompts/pr-review.md` is retained only for the markerless cloud PR-review path.

- **Lint/format coverage extended to the docs package + repo-wide Markdown** (`biome.json`, `package.json`, `.markdownlint.json`, `.markdownlint-cli2.jsonc`, `Makefile`, `AGENTS.md`): Biome's `files.includes` now covers `docs/**` JS/TS/JSON (astro config, mermaid theme, sidebar, worker, dev scripts) on top of the SDK and E2E harness — `.astro` templates and Markdown remain out of Biome's scope by design. Markdown across the whole repo is now linted by `markdownlint-cli2` (new root devDependency), wired into `make lint` / `make fix` next to Biome; rules live in `.markdownlint.json` (so editor extensions stay in sync) and file globs in `.markdownlint-cli2.jsonc`. `MD013` (line length), `MD033` (inline HTML — the README's theme-aware `<picture>`), and `MD060` (table column style) are disabled as ill-fitting here; `MD024` (duplicate headings) is exempted in `CHANGELOG.md` only, since Keep a Changelog repeats `### Added` / `Changed` / `Fixed` per version. `.mdx` is not linted (markdownlint parses CommonMark, not MDX's JSX).

- **Relicensed from MIT to Apache-2.0** (`LICENSE`, `clients/ts/LICENSE` (new), `NOTICE` (new), `README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SUPPORT.md`, `clients/ts/README.md`, `clients/ts/package.json`, `deployments/Dockerfile`, `deployments/Dockerfile.goreleaser`, `docs/src/components/Hero.astro`, `docs/src/content/docs/index.mdx`, `docs/src/content/docs/why-wavehouse.md`): the whole project moves from the MIT License to the Apache License 2.0 — a permissive→permissive change (Wave RF owns the copyright) that adds an explicit patent grant. Both `LICENSE` files carry the canonical Apache-2.0 text, a top-level `NOTICE` asserts `Copyright 2026 Wave RF`, and every textual reference is flipped: the README badge + text, CONTRIBUTING, SUPPORT, both Dockerfiles' OCI `licenses` label, the docs site's hero/stat card, the JSON-LD schema.org `license` URL, and the comparison table.

- **Ingest worker batches and flushes per table** (`internal/ingest/worker.go`, `internal/ingest/worker_test.go`, `docs/src/content/docs/ingest-pipeline.md` (new), `docs/src/config/sidebar.ts`, `tests/e2e/sdk/*` , `tests/e2e/fixtures/`): closes #191. The worker previously kept a **single shared batch across all tables**, so a high-volume table tripping the 500-row size trigger could strand a low-volume table's rows in a batch that then waited out the 5s timer (and vice-versa) — the root cause of the `batching.test.ts` CI flake.
  - **Per-table batching.** A single JetStream consumer now feeds a `dispatchLoop` that parses just enough to route each message to a per-table goroutine (`tableLoop`), lazily spawned on first sight of a table. Each table has its own batch, deadline timer, and size trigger, so one table's traffic never delays another's. State is single-owner/lock-free (no mutexes on the hot path); flushes hand the goroutine a private row snapshot so the race detector stays clean.
  - **Pipelined flushes with a bounded in-flight count (coalescing).** At most **one** insert runs per table at a time; the next batch accumulates while the current one is in flight, and a size/timer trigger that fires during a flush is latched and runs the moment the slot frees. Overflow coalesces into a larger insert when a table is hot — bigger batches under load, which is exactly what avoids ClickHouse "too many parts" without a flush-rate floor. `maxBatch`/`maxWait` are now struct fields (overridable in tests); `ackWait`/`maxAckPending`/`pullMaxMessages` invariants are documented. Background `DoubleAck`s are tracked on a separate `ackWg` so the fsync-bound acks stay off the insert path, and shutdown drains bottom-up (table loops → acks) under a single deadline.
  - **New contributor doc** `docs/src/content/docs/ingest-pipeline.md` (linked in the sidebar): a deep-dive on the goroutine/channel/timer topology, the `tableBatcher` state machine, the three contexts, shutdown ordering, backpressure knobs, and the path to multi-instance scale.
  - **E2E table isolation.** Each e2e test file now owns its own ClickHouse tables (`clicks_<suite>`/`events_<suite>`/`users_<suite>`, generated from `tests/e2e/sdk/tables.ts`), bootstrapped by `setup.ts`; the static `tests/e2e/fixtures/00*_*.sql` files are removed (DDL now lives in `tables.ts`). Cross-file data contamination is structurally impossible, and the `dlq.test.ts` 5s sleep workaround is gone. Running the files in **parallel** (dropping `maxWorkers: 1`) remains deferred (tracked in #214 — per-table policy storage) — see the "Deferred" section of the ingest-pipeline doc — because several files still read-modify-write the single global policy document.

- **Health endpoints aligned to the current Kubernetes convention — `/livez` + `/readyz` canonical, `/healthz` a permanent alias, `/health` + `/ready` deprecated — plus a new public `GET /v1/health` SDK liveness ping** (`internal/api/router.go`, `internal/api/router_test.go`, `internal/api/health.go`, `internal/api/health_test.go`, `internal/api/boot_chain_test.go`, `internal/config/config.go`, `internal/config/config_test.go`, `cmd/wavehouse/main.go`, `cmd/wavehouse/health.go`, `cmd/wavehouse/health_test.go`, `internal/discovery/discovery.go`, `internal/discovery/discovery_test.go`, `scripts/orchestrator/main.go`, `deployments/Dockerfile`, `deployments/Dockerfile.goreleaser`, `tests/integration/boot_resilience_test.go`, `tests/e2e/sdk/setup.ts`, `tests/e2e/sdk/admin.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/sdk/auth.test.ts`, `clients/ts/src/{sys,types,index,errors,errors.test,namespaces.test,http.test}.ts`, `docs/src/content/docs/{api,deployment,architecture,development,sdk}.md`). Addresses #144 (part 1).
  - **Canonical names.** Liveness is now `GET /livez` and readiness `GET /readyz` — the split the kube-apiserver adopted when it deprecated the older conflated `/healthz` (one endpoint can't distinguish "restart me" from "stop routing to me"). `/healthz` is kept as a **permanent alias** of `/livez` (it's the most widely-recognized name); `/health` and `/ready` are **deprecated aliases** for the v0.1.x line, **scheduled for removal in v0.2.0**. Handlers and response bodies are unchanged: `/livez` returns `{"status":"ok"}` (or `{"status":"degraded",…}` + 503 while boot-time schema discovery is still failing), `/readyz` returns `{"status":"ready"}` (or `{"status":"not ready","error":…}` + 503); kubelet only reads the status code, the bodies are for humans.
  - **New `GET /v1/health` (public, content-free).** A liveness ping returning a bodiless `200`/`503` (no JSON to encode, nothing to cache) with the same boot-aware logic as `/livez` but no ClickHouse check. It's deliberately a `/v1` API route rather than a Kubernetes probe path: operators may filter `/livez`/`/readyz`/`/healthz` out at the reverse proxy, so the SDK relies on `/v1/health` staying public for its "is the server online" check. Readiness-based load balancing remains the proxy/LB's job (via `/readyz`), not the client's — so no `/v1/ready` was added.
  - **Tooling repointed.** WaveHouse's own probes target the canonical `/livez` so they don't ride a removable alias: the distroless `health` subcommand (and its Docker `HEALTHCHECK`), the E2E orchestrator's startup wait, and the SDK E2E setup probe. The `prometheus.path` reserved-collision check now covers `/livez`/`/readyz` plus the three aliases, and the tracing span-skip middleware excludes all five probe paths and `/v1/health`.
  - **SDK (breaking).** The `sys` namespace now exposes only a content-free server-online check. `wh.sys.health()` calls `GET /v1/health` and returns `Result<void>` (was `Result<Health>` against `/health`) — branch on `error`, not `data`. **`wh.sys.ready()` was removed**: readiness is a per-call ClickHouse query that belongs to the load balancer / reverse proxy, not the client, and `/readyz` may be filtered at the proxy — probe `/readyz` directly for k8s/LB. The now-unused `Health` and `Ready` types were dropped from the public exports.
  - **SDK DX.** `Result<T>` gained an `ok: boolean` discriminant so callers can branch with `if (result.ok)` (mirroring `fetch`'s `Response.ok`) instead of checking `error === null` — cleaner for content-free results like `health()`. Additive and backward-compatible: `data`/`error` are unchanged, so existing checks keep working.
  - **Deferred (issue #144 part 2):** per-dependency drill-down probes (NATS, ClickHouse, schema, DLQ) and the richer aggregate `/readyz` body were intentionally left out of this release to keep the change small.

- **Docs site auto-deploys to `wavehouse.dev` from the CI job, and is driven from the root `Makefile` through the pnpm workspace** (`Makefile`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `docs/wrangler.jsonc`, `docs/scripts/branding/` (moved from `scripts/branding/`), `AGENTS.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/development.md`, `docs/scripts/screenshot.mjs`, `.github/actions/setup-env/action.yml`). Three threads, one PR:
  - **Driven from the root Makefile via the pnpm workspace.** `docs/` (package `wavehouse-docs`) is a pnpm workspace package built and served straight from the root `Makefile` with `pnpm --filter wavehouse-docs run <script>` — no sub-Makefile. Root targets are verb-first and slot into the normal `make help` sections: `build-docs`, `dev-docs`, `preview-docs`, `branding-docs`, `clean-docs`, plus a hidden `install-playwright-docs` helper that lazily fetches the Chromium build `rehype-mermaid` + `starlight-links-validator` need (the `--with-deps` apt step gated on `$CI`). The SDK (`clients/ts/`) and E2E harness (`tests/e2e/sdk/`) are workspace packages too, driven the same way (`build-ts`, `test-ts`, …), and `make ci` / `make tools` / `make build-all` call these targets directly.
  - **Branding pipeline relocated.** `scripts/branding/{mark.svg,lockup.svg,og.template.svg,generate.sh}` → `docs/scripts/branding/` since it's a docs-only concern; `generate.sh` now resolves the repo root via `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` instead of a hard-coded relative climb, so future moves don't re-break it. `make branding-docs` is the canonical entry point from root.
  - **Auto-deploy from the CI job.** Cloudflare Workers Builds can't build this site — `rehype-mermaid` renders diagrams to themed SVG at build time via headless Chromium, and the Workers Builds image has no browser (and no root to apt-install one), so `astro build` fails there. Since `make ci` already builds `docs/dist/` on a runner with a cached Chromium, the deploy runs as tail steps of `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, reusing that artifact and gated on the whole pipeline passing: push to `main` runs `wrangler deploy` (production → `wavehouse.dev`); PR branches run `wrangler versions upload`, which publishes a per-version preview URL of the form `<version-prefix>-wavehouse-docs.wave-rf.workers.dev` and posts it as a sticky PR comment. Deploys skip when no docs-affecting files changed and on fork PRs. Requires `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` + `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` repo secrets; Cloudflare Workers Builds must stay disconnected from the `wavehouse-docs` Worker so it doesn't double-deploy. `docs/wrangler.jsonc` gains the `routes` block for the `wavehouse.dev` custom domain, `workers_dev: true` + `preview_urls: true` so the per-version preview URLs keep working, `observability: { enabled: true, head_sampling_rate: 1 }`, `upload_source_maps: true`, and a `$schema` pointer for editor validation; `compatibility_date` bumped from `2025-01-01` to `2025-11-01`. The worker (`docs/worker/index.ts`, delegating to `cloudflare-md-router`) deploys alongside the static assets so `Accept: text/markdown` content negotiation works in production, not just under `wrangler dev`. AGENTS.md §"Repository Automation" documents the deploy mechanism so future maintainers know where to look.

- **Go toolchain requirement bumped to 1.26.4** (`go.mod`): the `go` directive moves from `1.26.3` to `1.26.4` so CI's `setup-go` (which reads `go.mod` via `go-version-file`) installs a Go whose standard library clears the `govulncheck` findings GO-2026-5037 / GO-2026-5038 / GO-2026-5039 (fixed in 1.26.4); local builds were already on 1.26.4. Patch-level toolchain bump only — no source changes — and the released binaries pick up the patched stdlib too.

- **Polish on the `/v1/ops/query` HTTP proxy from PR review** (`internal/api/query.go`, `internal/api/query_test.go`, `docs/src/content/docs/api.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/architecture.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/sdk.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/configuration.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/deployment.md`): tightening fixes from a clean-context review on PR #164.
  - **Status mapping split.** ClickHouse 4xx responses now map to HTTP 400 (caller-fault, the SQL was bad) and 5xx responses map to HTTP 502 (gateway-fault, the upstream had a problem), instead of every non-200 collapsing to 500. Admin tooling that retries on 5xx-but-not-4xx and dashboards that classify SLO violations get back the signal they need.
  - **`Content-Type` pass-through on inline `FORMAT`.** The fallback "ClickHouse returned a non-JSON body" branch (triggered by `FORMAT CSV` / `FORMAT Pretty` / etc., which override the URL-level `default_format=JSON`) now passes through the upstream `Content-Type` instead of stamping `application/json`. Confirmed empirically that the inline FORMAT wins at the protocol level; SDK consumers doing `await response.json()` would previously have crashed on the mislabeled CSV.
  - **Single source of truth for the deadline.** Dropped the duplicate `http.Client{Timeout: 30s}` — the per-request `context.WithTimeout(r.Context(), 30s)` already bounds the whole exchange including the body read, and the client-level Timeout silently capped any longer inbound context.
  - **Response-size cap (64 MiB).** Wrapped `io.ReadAll(resp.Body)` in an `io.LimitReader(_, maxCHResponseBytes+1)` with a 64 MiB cap, returning 502 on overflow. Admin-only endpoint, but a runaway `SELECT *` could still pin RAM on the API server, and a clear error beats a silent OOM.
  - **Inbound-body cap (16 MiB).** Wrapped `r.Body` in `http.MaxBytesReader(w, _, maxRequestBodyBytes)` for symmetry with the response cap. Oversize returns 413 with `"request body exceeded N bytes"` — distinct from `"invalid json"` so admin scripts can tell "you sent garbage" from "you sent too much."
  - **`Cache-Control: no-store` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` set unconditionally at handler entry**, so error responses carry both headers too — not only the 200 path.
  - **Test allocation pruned.** Added unexported `maxResponseBytes` / `maxRequestBytes` override fields on `QueryHandler` so size-cap tests can pin the overflow path with a tiny body instead of allocating tens of MiB per run.
  - Tests: `TestQueryHandler_ForwardsCHError` becomes table-driven across 4xx/5xx → 400/502 mapping; `TestQueryHandler_ForwardsRawBodyAndContentType` (renamed) now also asserts the upstream MIME passes through; `TestQueryHandler_ResponseSizeCap` and `TestQueryHandler_RequestBodyCap` are new and pin the 64 MiB / 16 MiB safety nets. A shared `assertSecurityHeaders` helper checks `Cache-Control: no-store` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` on every response path (200, 4xx CH error, 5xx CH error, 413 oversize-request, 502 oversize-response, FORMAT pass-through).

- **BREAKING: `POST /v1/ops/query` is now a thin HTTP proxy to ClickHouse, not a `clickhouse-go` Query/Exec wrapper** (`internal/api/query.go`, `internal/api/query_test.go`, `internal/api/clickhouse_exec.go` (new), `internal/api/clickhouse_exec_test.go` (new), `cmd/wavehouse/main.go`, `clients/ts/src/sql.ts`, `clients/ts/src/client.ts`, `clients/ts/src/namespaces.test.ts`, `tests/integration/query_test.go`, `docs/src/content/docs/api.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/architecture.md`). The handler now POSTs the SQL string verbatim to `<scheme>://<host>:<http_port>/?default_format=JSON&date_time_output_format=iso&database=<db>` with `X-ClickHouse-User` / `X-ClickHouse-Key` auth headers (matching the ingest worker's convention), reads ClickHouse's response, and forwards just the `data` array (or `[]` for mutations that return an empty body, or a JSON error envelope of shape `{"error":"<trimmed ClickHouse message>"}` for non-200 responses — the upstream plain-text body is wrapped inside the standard error envelope, not forwarded raw). Closes the `isMutation` / `mutationVerbs` / `containsMutationVerbAtTopLevel` / `stripLeadingSQLComments` / `transformRow` ~150-LOC machinery that lived in `query.go` to classify verbs and route between `driver.Exec` and `driver.Query`. Those primitives are still needed by the structured-query and pipes handlers (clickhouse-go's native Query() errors on no-result-set statements), so they moved into a new `internal/api/clickhouse_exec.go` and are called via `executeCHQuery(ctx, conn, sql, params)` — pipes and structured-query no longer instantiate a temporary `QueryHandler` just to borrow `executeQuery`. Three wins: (a) any ClickHouse-accepted SQL works, including multi-statement input (`SELECT 1; TRUNCATE t`) and arbitrary DDL/DML/SYSTEM verbs (current and future) without WaveHouse code changes; (b) ClickHouse's own error messages reach the admin verbatim — what they want from an escape hatch; (c) the handler is ~50 LOC of forward-and-shape-response with no SQL-parsing surface. **Breaking implications:** the request body no longer accepts a `params` array (ClickHouse's HTTP interface uses named `{name:Type}` binding, not positional `?`). Inline literals into the SQL string, or use the structured query endpoint for safe binding from user inputs. The TypeScript SDK's `sql()` method drops its `params?: unknown[]` argument; callers of `client.sql(sql, params)` need to inline. `NewQueryHandler` signature changes from `(driver.Conn)` to `(endpoint, username, password, database string)`. The response also gains a `Cache-Control: no-store` header so no downstream layer caches raw-SQL results. New tests in `clickhouse_exec_test.go` keep `isMutation` + Exec/Query routing + the `transformRow` UUID/time conversions covered for the structured-query and pipes paths that still need them; new tests in `query_test.go` pin the proxy contract (SQL forwarded verbatim, data array extracted, empty-body → `[]`, ClickHouse error forwarded, security headers set on every response path, request context cancel propagates).

- **`POST /v1/ops/query` no longer caches, singleflights, or bypasses cache for mutations** (`internal/api/query.go`, `internal/api/query_test.go`, `internal/api/cache_key.go` (new), `internal/api/cache_key_test.go` (new), `cmd/wavehouse/main.go`, `docs/src/content/docs/api.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/architecture.md`). **Note (largely superseded — see the HTTP-proxy entry above):** the structural details below (`QueryHandler` struct shape, `NewQueryHandler(conn)` signature, in-handler verb classifier, `X-Cache: MISS` header) describe the state immediately after the cache-strip commit. The subsequent HTTP-proxy refactor changed `NewQueryHandler` to `(endpoint, username, password, database string)`, dropped the `driver.Conn` field, moved the verb classifier to `internal/api/clickhouse_exec.go` for the structured-query/pipes consumers that still need it, and uses `Cache-Control: no-store` in place of `X-Cache`. The cache-strip motivation below still stands; the historical implementation specifics follow. With the endpoint now an admin-only escape hatch (see the Security entry below), the L1/L2 + singleflight + mutation-cache-bypass machinery added in the original raw-SQL handler is dead weight: admin calls are infrequent, often mutations, often one-shot, so the cache hit rate is effectively zero and singleflight has no concurrent-request herd to collapse. `QueryHandler` shrinks to `{ CHConn driver.Conn }`; `Handle` is parse → executeQuery → marshal → write. `NewQueryHandler(conn)` is the new signature (was `NewQueryHandler(conn, *cache.TieredCache, time.Duration)`). The `X-Cache: HIT/MISS` response header is gone from this endpoint — every response is a fresh ClickHouse round-trip, no header needed to disambiguate. The verb-classification machinery (`isMutation`, `mutationVerbs`, the comment-aware CTE parser) STAYS; it's correctness, not performance — clickhouse-go's `driver.Query()` errors on statements that return no result set, so we still route mutations through `driver.Exec`. The shared `queryCacheKey` helper moved to a new `internal/api/cache_key.go` since it's now consumed only by the structured query (`/v1/tables/{table}/query`) and pipes (`/v1/pipes/{name}`) handlers, which keep their caching + singleflight intact — those are the high-QPS read paths where the machinery earns its keep. Two now-obsolete tests removed: `TestQueryCacheKey_Deterministic` (moved to a fresh `cache_key_test.go` since the helper still has callers) and `TestQueryHandler_MutationBypassesCache` (no cache to bypass). The mutation-routing tests (`TestExecuteQuery_MutationRoutesToExec`, `TestExecuteQuery_SelectRoutesToQuery`) stay — they pin the still-required Exec-vs-Query classification.

- **JWT auth middleware polish from PR review** (`internal/auth/auth.go`, `internal/auth/auth_test.go`, `docs/src/content/docs/api.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/sdk.md`): docs + test follow-ups from review of the RBAC work; no behavior change.
  - Documented that the `Authorization` header takes precedence over the `?token=` query param: the query param is only a fallback for header-less clients (browser `EventSource`), so a token in that more log-leakable URL never overrides an explicit header credential, and it is stripped from the URL after extraction (`api.md`). `sdk.md` gains a "How the token is transmitted" note — header on REST, `?token=` on SSE at that point, server prefers the header when both are present. The SDK's stream transport sends the header too as of the streaming entry above; the token is no longer placed in the URL.
  - Replaced the parse-path TODO with an in-code comment: the middleware returns no error by design (authentication is decoupled from authorization). A failed/invalid/expired parse — and the effectively-unreachable non-`MapClaims` branch — all fall through to the single fail-safe that records the token error via `WithAuthError`, leaving the request roleless for a downstream gate to surface as `401` vs `403`.
  - Added `TestMiddleware_JWKSEmptyKeySet_TokenDoesNotAuthenticate`: an empty `{"keys":[]}` set constructs at boot but validates no token at request time, so every token falls back to the roleless `default_role` (fail-closed). It signs an EdDSA token so the token passes the JWKS algorithm allowlist and actually reaches the empty key set, rather than being rejected by the alg gate first.

- **Config and policy examples in every supported format, plus reference-page chrome odds and ends** (`docs/src/content/docs/{configuration.mdx,access-control.mdx}` (renamed from `.md`), `docs/src/content/docs/{architecture,development,pipes,claude-code}.md`, `docs/src/styles/global.css`): the example config file is now YAML / Environment synced tabs (`syncKey="cfg"`; every `WH_*` name cross-checked against `internal/config/config.go` — the config FILE is YAML-only by struct tags, so no JSON tab there), and the complete policy example is YAML / JSON synced tabs (`syncKey="policy"`; the JSON twin machine-verified equal to the YAML). Code frames that represent real files gained `title=` headers (`config.yaml`, `policy.yaml`, `.env`, `~/.claude.json`, …) and every frame — terminals included — now shows a quiet language badge at its top-right (inside the title bar where one exists; ScrollHints copies `pre[data-language]` up to the frame since CSS `attr()` can't cross elements; plain-text-family frames stay unlabeled, and the badge yields to the hover copy button on headerless frames). A follow-up round, on review feedback: every substantive policy fragment on the access-control page is now a YAML/JSON tab group (7 groups total, each JSON twin machine-parsed equal to its YAML), the configuration tables returned to separate YAML Key / Env Var columns (the stacked merge predated wide-table support; 4-column tables now ride the breakout track), code-format tab groups DOCK onto their code frame (flush bottom edge, shared corner — a floating segmented control above an unrelated box read as disconnected), external-link `↗` markers extend to prose and footer links (absolute http(s) hrefs are off-site by construction), and the diagram zoom chip became a proper masked stroke icon on a translucent blurred chip instead of a `⤢` text glyph. A fifth round (review feedback + an automated content/visual sweep) then: scoped the cluster-stroke padding to diagram scrollers only (on table cards it had opened a page-bg seam between the card border and the header band), cleared the zoom stage's leftover edge-fade attributes on every open (a prior 1:1 pan session painted a phantom fade on the next fitting diagram), settled the docked-group chrome (no `title=`/filename comments inside format tabs — the lead-in prose names the file, the tab names the format; the configuration Environment tab became dotenv-style `ini` so it stops rendering as a titled terminal), added the missing `cfg` tab groups (access-control's policy-bootstrap fragment, pipes' `.sql`-seeding fragment — pipes.md → `.mdx`), pretty-printed the wide single-line JSON wire examples (api.md responses; the SSE `data:` lines deliberately stay wire-accurate single lines and wrap visually), manually wrapped >100ch comment/log lines across deployment/architecture/sdk pages (the WARN/ERROR log examples now break with hanging indents under `wrap=false`), excluded prev/next pagination cards and the footer brand sentence from the external-link `↗` (a second glyph inside the Next card read as broken), and made the slim footer wrap deliberately below ~86rem (links become their own left-aligned row; the squeezed pane can't fit the one-line bar there). A sixth, dedicated pass killed the recurring "shadow with nothing to scroll" artifact by construction rather than by threshold: a WRAPPED code block (`pre.wrap`) can never meaningfully scroll sideways (pre-wrap + break-word guarantee every line fits — anything scrollWidth reports past clientWidth is hanging wrap-point whitespace or zoom/font-timing rounding), so the edge-fade updater now hard-strips both fade attributes from wrapped pres unconditionally; classifications also re-derive once `document.fonts.ready` resolves, so fallback-font metrics can't go stale. Code blocks then adopted the tables' earn-the-width rule (the PR author's "if it's going to wrap anyway, no point being so wide" observation): a block widens only when its longest line actually FITS the wide track (measured batched, nowrap), or when it's `wrap=false` and scrolls — a block whose longest line exceeds even the full pane keeps the prose width instead of stranding two words on a continuation line at maximum width. Long trailing comments that previously forced the wrap moved above their lines across access-control/deployment/development/sdk examples. A dedicated verification agent then proved the artifact dead: 42,696 element scans — 18 pages × 141 widths (320–2560, step 16, resize-triggered re-classification), every tab option clicked at 3 widths, the full zoom lifecycle (fit / 1:1 / pan / toggle-back / next-diagram) on 16 diagrams at 2 widths, soft-navigation chains, and blocked- and delayed-webfont variants — zero violations of the invariant (fade attribute ⇒ real overflow > 12px, and never on a wrapped pre). A seventh round, on CodeRabbit review: code-ONLY tab groups (the YAML/JSON/env switchers) now earn the wide track as a unit — `classifyCode()` previously scanned only direct `.expressive-code` children, so tabbed samples could never receive `data-wh-wide`; digging in exposed a second, older hole — the natural-width probe set `white-space: pre` on the `pre`, but EC applies wrapping on the descendant code spans (`pre.wrap .ec-line .code`), so the probe measured the WRAPPED width and every default-`wrap` block was unwidenable (only `wrap=false` blocks ever took the wide track); the wrap class itself now comes off for the batched measurement, which also lets the long-lined wrap blocks across the api/development/sdk pages widen as the sixth-pass rule always intended (verified: 19 pages × 5 widths, zero overflow / fade-on-wrap / stale-fade violations); all panels are measured (inactive ones get a momentary inline `display:block` — layout never reaches paint mid-batch) and the group pins to its widest panel’s natural width so the frame doesn’t resize across tab switches (groups with prose in any panel keep the reading measure, and a tab switch now refreshes the edge fades — a pre revealed from `hidden` measured 0×0 when fades were last stamped). The bare `table` selector left ScrollHints’ scroller list (after wrapping, tables scroll on their `.wh-table-wrap` — a fade stamped on the non-scrolling `<table>` itself could never clear), the phone Mermaid wrapper-scroller rule extends to `class`/`state` diagrams (they had the 540px no-JS min-width floor without the matching `overflow-x: auto`), and the search-dialog backdrop dropped a reintroduced hand-written `-webkit-backdrop-filter` twin (the same LightningCSS author-managed-prefix trap documented at the glass header — the build emits the twin itself). Bare issue references in prose became links (#97, #223, #224, #279). The mobile icon-only search trigger joins the menu button's circular control family (it rendered as an unstyled square), and external sidebar links (Contributing Guide, Discussions, …) carry a `↗` marker.

- **SDK docs split into six topic pages, with package-manager-synced install tabs** (`docs/src/content/docs/sdk/{index.mdx,queries.md,streaming.md,pipes.md,admin.md,reference.md}` (new), `docs/src/content/docs/sdk.md` (deleted), `docs/src/config/sidebar.ts`, `docs/src/components/Hero.astro`): the 806-line monolith becomes `/sdk` (overview: install, quick start, client setup, `Result<T>`, section cards) plus `/sdk/{queries,streaming,pipes,admin,reference}`, under a nested "TypeScript SDK" sidebar group (which also keeps the llms.txt page ordering coherent — the LLM outputs read the sidebar). Topic-first URLs are the multi-language plan on record: when a second SDK language lands, the shared usage pages grow `<Tabs syncKey="lang">` code tabs and each language gets its own setup/caveats page, so the topic URLs never churn. Installation became `<Tabs syncKey="pkg">` (pnpm / npm / yarn / bun / deno / CDN), and the landing hero's click-to-copy install chip now follows that synced choice — Starlight persists the selected tab label in `localStorage` under its syncKey, the hero script maps it to the matching one-liner (CDN and anything unknown keep the pnpm default). Every inbound link from other pages already pointed at bare `/sdk` (still the overview); the three intra-page `#streaming` anchors became `/sdk/streaming` links, and `starlight-links-validator` gates the rest. A follow-up adds an "Imports & Runtimes" section to the overview — import-line tabs synced to the same `pkg` choice (the CDN reader sees the esm.sh import everywhere, including a fully-tabbed Quick Start), Node CJS `require()` and the `WaveHouse` IIFE global documented from the shipped exports map, and a verified runtime-support note (streaming needed an `EventSource` polyfill in Node at that point — the global was still flag-gated as of Node 26, checked empirically; non-streaming worked on Node 18+). The polyfill requirement is gone as of the `fetch` transport above. Each topic page points CDN readers at the section.

- **Pre-push CI marker is tree-keyed, not commit-keyed** (`Makefile`, `.githooks/pre-push`, `.claude/hooks/agent-bash-gate.sh`, `AGENTS.md`). The `make ci → git add → git commit → git push` flow no longer false-positive-blocks — committing advances HEAD but doesn't change the tree CI validated. `make ci` writes `tmp/ci-passed-tree-<TREE-SHA>` computed against a throwaway `GIT_INDEX_FILE` (so untracked files are captured without touching the real index); pre-push and the agent-bash-gate resolve each pushed ref's `^{tree}` and match. Skipped on CI runners (they don't push). `review-passed` stays commit-keyed — a new commit needs a new review.

- **Worktrunk seeds new worktrees from main** (`.config/wt.toml`). Added `wt step copy-ignored` to `post-start` ahead of `make tools`, so `wt switch --create <branch>` copies `.bin/` and the three `node_modules/` trees (~1 GB combined) from main instead of cold-starting `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` × 3. `[step.copy-ignored] exclude` keeps `tmp/`, `data/`, and vitest's `coverage/` out.

- **Claude PR review is now manual-only** (`.github/workflows/claude-review.yml`, `AGENTS.md`): dropped the `pull_request` trigger (was firing on opened/synchronize/reopened/ready_for_review) so the workflow only runs on `@claude` or `/review` comments from trusted reviewers, or `workflow_dispatch`. "Claude review" was never a required status check (the ruleset gates on `Admin approval` / `CI` / `PR housekeeping`), so this changes who pays the action budget — every push no longer triggers a review automatically; reviewers explicitly opt in via mention after they've looked at the diff themselves. Falls out: the workflow header's `pull_request_target` OIDC paragraph dropped (only applied to the auto-trigger path), concurrency's `cancel-in-progress` is now unconditionally `false` (manual triggers should always queue, never cancel an in-flight review), the workflow-level `if:` loses its `pull_request` branch + dependabot-author exclusion, and the gate-step `case` drops the `pull_request)` arm + its two `PR_EVENT_*` env vars (`gh pr view --jq .headRefOid` is hoisted out of the case since both remaining paths need it identically). The trust gate stays — it's *more* load-bearing under manual-only, since `issue_comment` runs with full repo secrets and a trusted reviewer commenting `@claude` on a fork PR would otherwise check out untrusted fork code with write tokens. AGENTS.md synced in three places: §"Review Response" step 6 (Claude moved from synchronize-rerun to PR-comment-mention), the review tooling reference table (Claude row now "Manual-only" with "No" in the re-runs column), and §"Repository Automation" §"Code review" entry (rewritten to lead with manual-only).

- **BREAKING:** the SSE streaming endpoint replaced `?topic=` with `?table=` (issue #100, option B). The NATS subject convention no longer leaks into the public HTTP API; the server builds `ingest.<table>` internally. SSE returns `400 Bad Request` when `?table=` is missing or fails the `^[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_]*$` safe-identifier check (the same regex `internal/ingest` and `internal/query` use for SQL identifiers — crucially this rejects NATS wildcard characters `*` and `>` before they reach the gap-fill `FilterSubject`).

- TypeScript SDK (`@wavehouse/sdk`) updated to the new contract: `SSEOptions` carries `table` instead of `topic`. The high-level SDK API (`wh.from('clicks').stream()`) is unchanged.

- Doc-sync for the `?topic=` → `?table=` rename: `docs/src/content/docs/getting-started.md` quick-start now shows `?table=clicks` (the previous `?topic=ingest.clicks` example would 400 against the new contract).

- **Boot-time schema discovery no longer crashes the binary** (`cmd/wavehouse/main.go`, `internal/api/health.go`, `internal/discovery/discovery.go`, `docs/src/content/docs/api.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/deployment.md`). Previously, any error from the initial `SchemaRegistry.Refresh` — connection-refused, missing database, transient network blip — caused `cmd/wavehouse` to call `os.Exit(1)`. The supervisor would restart the process every ~10s in an unbounded loop, port 8080 never bound, and operators got `connection refused` on probes even when ClickHouse was otherwise healthy. Now the first Refresh failure is non-fatal: a new `api.BootState` is set with the diagnostic, the server still binds `:8080`, and a background goroutine calls the new `SchemaRegistry.RetryRefresh(ctx, 2s, 60s, onAttempt)` with exponential backoff until success or shutdown. While `BootState.Err()` is non-nil, `/health` returns 503 with `{"status":"degraded","error":"…"}` so an operator can `curl /health` to learn why the gateway isn't ready to serve traffic instead of grepping a restart-loop log; `/ready` returns 503 with `{"status":"not ready",…}` for the same reason. Once `BootState.Set(nil)` fires (either from the initial sync `Refresh` or from `SchemaRegistry.RetryRefresh` success), `/health` stays at `200 OK` for the rest of the process lifetime — that flip is sticky and reflects "boot completed once." `/ready`, by contrast, remains conditional on current ClickHouse reachability: it can flip back to `503` on transient runtime blips after boot, since readiness is a probe of "can I serve traffic right now," not "did I ever boot." Resolves #95.

- **Frontend toolchain pinned: pnpm 10.33 → 11.1.3, Node 22 LTS via `.nvmrc`** (`.nvmrc`, `.github/actions/setup-env/action.yml`, `clients/ts/package.json`, `clients/ts/pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `docs/package.json`, `docs/pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `tests/e2e/sdk/package.json`, `tests/e2e/sdk/pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `docs/src/content/docs/development.md`). Closes #160. Three TS workspaces (`clients/ts`, `tests/e2e/sdk`, `docs`) plus the `setup-env` composite action bump from `pnpm@10.33.0` to `pnpm@11.1.3`. pnpm 11 deprecated the `pnpm` field in `package.json` — `onlyBuiltDependencies` moved into a new per-workspace `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and was renamed to `allowBuilds:` with a map-of-booleans syntax (each workspace ships its own file even though they're not a single monorepo — having them in-tree shadows any stray ancestor `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, which is how pnpm 11 walks the directory tree). `clients/ts` and `tests/e2e/sdk` allow `esbuild`; `docs` additionally allows `sharp` (Astro image pipeline) and `workerd` (Cloudflare Workers runtime). New repo-root `.nvmrc` pins Node 22 LTS — matches the `setup-env` action's existing `node-version: "22"` — and `docs/package.json#engines.node` bumps from `>=20.0` to `>=22.0` so corepack / volta warn local devs before CI does. The original issue's open question about `electron-winstaller` resolved to a contamination from a stray `pnpm-workspace.yaml` in the developer's home directory, not a real WaveHouse transitive — correctly omitted from `allowBuilds`. The Node 26 Vitest crash mentioned in the issue is no longer reproducible after the recent `vitest@^4` bump in `clients/ts` (#103); the `.nvmrc` still lands because pinning has independent value (CI parity + Node-major-surprise guard).

- **pnpm workspace consolidation: a single root `pnpm-workspace.yaml` + lockfile** (`pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, root `package.json`; removed `clients/ts/pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `docs/pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `tests/e2e/sdk/pnpm-workspace.yaml` and their per-project `pnpm-lock.yaml` files): the three TS projects (`clients/ts`, `docs`, `tests/e2e/sdk`) are now real workspace members under one root install with a shared `catalog:` for common dev dependencies. The in-repo root workspace file still shadows any stray ancestor `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, and the `minimumReleaseAge` supply-chain guard is preserved at the root.

- **Single root `Makefile`; removed the per-subproject `docs/Makefile`** (`Makefile`, `docs/Makefile` deleted): one top-level Makefile drives Go plus all TS workspaces with verb-first targets and a first-class SDK target; docs build/lint/format now run from the repo root instead of `make -C docs`.

- **`engines.node` normalized to `>=22` across every workspace `package.json`** (`package.json`, `clients/ts/package.json`, `docs/package.json`, `tests/e2e/sdk/package.json`): the repo root and `clients/ts` still declared `>=18` even though CI, `.nvmrc`, and `docs` already required Node 22 — the declared floor now matches the `.nvmrc` pin everywhere (`tests/e2e/sdk` previously declared none). The redundant `packageManager` field was dropped from `docs/package.json` since the root `package.json` is authoritative for the workspace.

- **Breaking:** `mq.Message.Ack()` and `mq.Message.Nak()` now return `error` (previously no return value) and no longer accept a `context.Context`.

- **Breaking:** Added `mq.Message.DoubleAck(ctx context.Context) error` for synchronous, server-confirmed acknowledgments. Use this for critical ingest paths (ClickHouse writes).

- Ingest worker now uses NATS `DoubleAck` for explicit server-side confirmation before finalizing batches.

- Ingest worker now strictly enforces `insert` and `delete` actions; unrecognized actions are safely rejected to the DLQ.

- **BREAKING: removed `WH_MQ_STREAM_NAME` / `mq.stream_name` — JetStream stream names are now hardcoded in `internal/mq/mq.go`.** The previous configurable stream name was a half-implemented multi-tenancy story from the external-NATS era: `cfg.MQ.StreamName` was threaded into `mq.NewEmbedded`, `api.EnsureDLQStream`, `ingest.StartIngestWorker`, and `api.NewDLQHandler`, but the **Sweeper, SSE handler, and WS handler** all called the hardcoded `mq.StreamName()` function (returning `"WAVEHOUSE"`) instead. Setting `WH_MQ_STREAM_NAME=PROD` would have created the stream as `PROD` but left the Sweeper looking up `WAVEHOUSE` — silent state-sync drift. Since WaveHouse is now fully embedded (each instance has its own NATS process), there's no other tenant to namespace against, and the config option was the inconsistent outlier next to the already-hardcoded `WAVEHOUSE_PIPES` (KV) and `WAVEHOUSE_POLICY` (KV) buckets. New `mq.DLQStreamName()` mirrors `mq.StreamName()` for symmetry. `mq.NewEmbedded`, `api.EnsureDLQStream`, `ingest.StartIngestWorker`, and `api.NewDLQHandler` all dropped their `streamName` / `baseStreamName` parameters; signature changes ripple through `cmd/wavehouse/main.go`, `tests/integration/setup_test.go`, and `internal/api/dlq_test.go`. `TestValidate_DefaultsStreamNameWhenEmpty` removed; `TestDLQStreamName` added. Removes the corresponding TODO entry in `TODO.md`.

- **BREAKING: replaced `WH_MQ_EMBEDDED_DIR` / `WH_DEDUPE_EMBEDDED_DIR` with single `WH_DATA_DIR`.** NATS lives at `<data_dir>/nats`, Pebble (when dedupe is enabled) at `<data_dir>/pebble`. The two old env vars and yaml keys (`mq.embedded_dir`, `dedupe.embedded_dir`) are removed — anything setting them needs to switch to `data_dir` / `WH_DATA_DIR`. Default is `./data`. Compose env (`deployments/compose/standalone.yaml`, `tests/e2e/compose.yaml`, `clients/ts/playground/compose.yaml`) updated to `WH_DATA_DIR=/app/data` with a `wavehouse-data:/app/data` volume; `scripts/orchestrator/main.go` updated to set `WH_DATA_DIR=tmp/data`. Both `Dockerfile` and `Dockerfile.goreleaser` pre-create only the parent dirs (`/app/data` and `/app/pipes`) owned by `nonroot:nonroot` — the binary mkdirs `nats/` and `pebble/` subdirectories itself when NATS/Pebble open their stores, so deeper pre-creation would buy nothing. `Dockerfile.goreleaser` uses a tiny `alpine:3` layout stage to do this since distroless has no shell.

- **BREAKING: renamed `WH_PIPES_DIRECTORY` → `WH_PIPES_DIR` and changed default from `./pipes` to empty.** Empty default skips the bootstrap entirely — most users will create pipes via the API. The pipes directory is now documented as **read-only at runtime**: it's a *seed* loaded into the NATS KV `WAVEHOUSE_PIPES` bucket on startup, not authoritative storage. Compose snippets in `docs/deployment.md` show the `:ro` bind-mount pattern (`./my-pipes:/app/pipes:ro`). `internal/pipes/pipes.go` already handled `directory == ""` by skipping the load — only the config and docs needed to change.

- **`project-orchestrator.yml` refactored to the set-once-assignees + mirrored-status model.** Assignees are now treated as per-card identity (Issue = implementer, PR = reviewer) rather than rotated across state transitions; statuses mirror across linked PR↔Issue pairs. Specific changes:
  - On bot-clean → Ready: linked issues now promoted to `In review` (was missing; previously only `re_requested` path moved issues).
  - Assignee and GitHub review-request are now applied together via a single composite action — board queue and GitHub notification stay in sync.
  - `Lint`, `Test`, `Integration Tests`, `SDK Tests` joined `Check`/`Build`/`Validate` in the bot-clean required-checks set, matching the updated ruleset.
  - Promotion guard ("don't yank In progress / In review / Done back to Ready") preserved.
  - ~250 lines of duplicate GraphQL/item-edit shell collapsed into composite-action calls. Full state-machine spec in `AGENTS.md §Task Board state machine`.
  - **Re-eval trigger set fixed.** The original `check_suite: completed` trigger silently never fired on internal PRs — GitHub suppresses `check_suite` events when the suite was created by a `GITHUB_TOKEN`-triggered workflow (standard case for our CI). Replaced with `workflow_run` watching the `CI` workflow, which is the documented chain-off-another-workflow mechanism and doesn't have the suppression gotcha. Also broadened the `pull_request_review: submitted` handler so `COMMENTED` reviews (which is what bots like Gemini post) also trigger a bot-clean re-eval — gives the state machine three independent paths (push, bot review, CI completion) to re-eval instead of effectively one.

- **Restored unit-test coverage threshold to 70%** in `.testcoverage.yml` (was temporarily lowered to 60% in #66) and removed the `exclude.paths` entries for `internal/dedupe`, `internal/mq`, and `internal/observability` now that those packages have `_test.go` files. `internal/testutil` remains excluded — it is test-only helper code. Measured total is **71.3%**, up from 65% with the old exclusions applied. CI/Makefile/AGENTS.md commentary updated to reflect the stable 70% bar. Closes #67.

- **`pr-title.yml` and `label.yml` now use only `pull_request_target`.** The dual-trigger (`pull_request` + `pull_request_target`) introduced in #69 was a transition: `pull_request_target` is on `main` and confirmed firing, so the `pull_request` half is dropped. Stops running each check twice on internal PRs and removes the sticky-comment TOCTOU race. Concurrency-group `event_name` suffix reverted with the trigger removal — only one event fires now.

- **`claude-review.yml` waits for required CI to settle and skips on red.** New first step polls the check-runs API for the PR's required checks (`Check`, `Build`, `Validate`, `Lint`, `Test`, `Integration Tests`) until they reach a terminal state, then short-circuits if any of them failed/cancelled/timed-out. Stops burning Claude review tokens on PRs the human will bounce back as "fix CI first." Re-pushing green CI re-triggers the review normally. (Polls *named* checks rather than using `gh pr checks --watch` — the latter would wait on every check including the Review job itself and deadlock.)

- **CI now runs SDK + E2E test jobs.** `sdk-test` runs `make test-sdk` (vitest unit tests in `clients/ts`) on every PR — gap surfaced by #63 where a Dependabot SDK bump ran zero TypeScript tests. `e2e` runs `make test-e2e` (vitest in `tests/sdk`, which spins up the full ClickHouse + WaveHouse compose stack via `setup.ts`) gated on `pull_request.draft == false` so WIP pushes don't pay Docker-build time. Both use SHA-pinned `actions/setup-node@v6.4.0` and pin Node 22 (the E2E streaming test exercises native WebSocket, which Node 20 doesn't provide). The `e2e` job is marked `continue-on-error: true` pending follow-up on two pre-existing bugs surfaced by wiring it in (a raw-SQL query that races Bento's ingest-batch flush, and the WebSocket polyfill already addressed by the Node-version bump).

- **Reviewer assignment, admin approval, and Task Board orchestration moved from CODEOWNERS to workflows.** Removed `.github/CODEOWNERS`; admin approval on every PR is now enforced by `.github/workflows/admin-approval.yml` as a required status check (fails unless Eric or Taite has an `APPROVED` review, Dependabot PRs bypass via `dependabot-automerge.yml`). Reviewer assignment + Task Board state orchestration handled by `.github/workflows/project-orchestrator.yml`: when a PR becomes bot-clean (required checks green + all review threads resolved), the workflow adds it to the Task Board (project #7), sets Status=`Ready`, and assigns the non-author admin to the PR's `assignees` field (not as a GitHub review-request — the board card + assignment is the single signal). Subsequent events drive a full state machine: `CHANGES_REQUESTED` review moves PR→`In review` and linked issue→`Ready`; author re-requesting review moves PR→`Ready` and issue→`In review`; merge handled by existing project automation. One manual step intentionally preserved: reviewer moves PR card `Ready`→`In progress` when they start reviewing. Ruleset updated: `require_code_owner_review: false` (no CODEOWNERS), `Admin approval` added to `required_status_checks`.

- **All three `cmd/` binaries use a `run() int` pattern** (`cmd/wavehouse`, `cmd/wavehouse-api`, `cmd/wavehouse-worker`, `tests/cmd/bento_pub`): `main()` now calls `os.Exit(run())` so deferred OTEL shutdown and resource `Close()` calls actually fire on error exit — previously every `os.Exit(1)` inside `main()` silently skipped them. All HTTP servers gained a `ReadHeaderTimeout: 10s` to close gosec G112 (Slowloris).

- **Errcheck sweep across `cmd/` and `internal/`**: wrapped deferred `Close` / `Shutdown` calls in `func() { _ = x.Close() }()` and acknowledged ignored errors on `json.Encoder.Encode`, `http.ResponseWriter.Write`, `fmt.Fprintf`, `rows.Close`, and NATS `m.Ack` / `m.Nak`. `jsInput.Read`'s Ack/Nak paths now log a warning on failure so lost acks are visible.

- **Narrowed `revive` ruleset in `.golangci.yml`** to an explicit list of semantically useful rules (`error-return`, `context-as-argument`, `var-naming`, etc.). Dropped the defaults `exported`, `package-comments`, and `unused-parameter` which together produced ~50 findings that were pure comment-style noise for internal-only packages. Easy to re-enable per-rule later if the team wants to require doc comments.

- **Unit test coverage threshold temporarily lowered 70% → 60%** in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `Makefile`, and added `.testcoverage.yml` with `exclude.paths` for packages that have no `_test.go` files (`internal/dedupe`, `internal/mq`, `internal/observability`, `internal/testutil`). Those packages get linked into test binaries and so appeared in the coverage profile at 0%, dragging the measured total to 54.7% even though the tested surface was at 64.9%. After the exclusions, measured total is **64.9%** vs a 60% threshold. Restoring the 70% target + deleting the exclusions is tracked in **#67**.

- **Debug `fmt.Println` / `fmt.Printf` removed from `cmd/*/main.go`** — replaced with `logger.Info` / `logger.Error` lines or folded into the existing startup log. Prevented unformatted plaintext lines from appearing interleaved with JSON logs in production (flagged by Claude's re-review of #66). Also dropped the hardcoded `172.18.240.1:4317` WSL-gateway default in `cmd/wavehouse-worker/main.go` — now defaults to `127.0.0.1:4317` like the other two binaries.

- **`goreleaser-action` CLI version pin**: `release.yml` now passes `version: "~> v2"` to `goreleaser-action` instead of `version: latest`. Picks up patch / minor GoReleaser bumps automatically but breaks loudly on a v3 major bump rather than silently changing release binaries. Flagged in Claude's post-merge review of #55.

- **`dependabot-automerge.yml` permissions kept at `contents: write` + `pull-requests: write`**: initially considered dropping `contents: write` per Claude's post-merge review of #55 (citing that `gh pr review` + `gh pr merge --auto` only need `pull-requests: write`). Copilot's review of #64 caught the edge case — when required checks are already green at the moment `gh pr merge --auto` runs, it performs an immediate squash merge, which needs tree-write. Keeping the broader scope to avoid silent failures in the all-green edge case.

- **Claude PR review prompt upgraded**: `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml` prompt restructured to follow Anthropic's canonical `pr-review-comprehensive` template (Code Quality / Security / Performance / Testing / Documentation) with an explicit "rigorous, skeptical staff engineer" tone directive, OWASP Top 10 walk for the security section with severity rubric (`CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW/NONE`), WaveHouse-specific concerns (Go concurrency, ClickHouse SQL-injection surfaces, AGENTS.md doc-sync rules), and Anthropic's noise-reduction filter ("post only the feedback you also deem noteworthy, keep feedback concise").

- **Issue template config**: `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/config.yml` disables blank issues and adds a Security Policy contact link that routes vulnerability reports away from public issues.

- **Repo labels — `area/*` rationalization**: Added `area/api`, `area/ingest`, `area/query`, `area/cache`, `area/dedupe`, `area/policy`, `area/pipes`, `area/sdk`, `area/docs`, `area/infra`. Renamed `Observability` → `area/observability` (preserves label on 17 historical issues). Added `agent`, `security`, `breaking-change`. Deleted unused hardware-org leftovers (`Hardware`, `Software`, `firmware`).

- **Supply-chain hardening — SHA-pinned GitHub Actions**: All `uses:` references in `ci.yml` and `release.yml` now pin to full-length commit SHAs with version comments (`actions/checkout`, `actions/setup-go`, `golangci/golangci-lint-action`, `vladopajic/go-test-coverage`, `docker/login-action`, `goreleaser/goreleaser-action`). Protects against tag-retargeting attacks on third-party actions. Dependabot tracks updates and will open PRs that bump both the SHA and the comment.

- **SECURITY.md refreshed**: JWT section now documents JWKS support (previously only mentioned HMAC), adds role-based access control detail, distinguishes raw-SQL vs structured-query permissions, and notes the supply-chain posture (SHA-pinned actions, govulncheck, Dependabot).

- **AGENTS.md extended**: New sections for repository automation tiers (triage / Gemini / Claude agent), governance files (CODEOWNERS intent, pointer-file discipline), and the workflow rule that adding an `internal/` package requires adding a matching `area/*` label and extending `triage.yml`'s area enumeration.

- **Hub wildcard support**: `Broadcast()` now matches NATS-style topic wildcards (`*` for one token, `>` for one-or-more remaining tokens) so SSE/WS clients subscribing to patterns like `ingest.>` receive events for all matching subjects. Includes dedup to prevent double-delivery.

- **Auth `?token=` query parameter fallback**: JWT can now be passed via `?token=<jwt>` query parameter for WebSocket and SSE connections where custom headers are not possible. The `Authorization` header takes precedence. Token is stripped from URL after extraction.

- **SSE `id:` field and `Last-Event-ID` reconnection**: SSE events now include an `id:` field set to `received_timestamp`, enabling native `EventSource` automatic reconnection. The `Last-Event-ID` request header overrides `?since=` for seamless gap-fill on reconnect.

- **WebSocket multiplexing**: WebSocket connections now support in-band JSON commands (`{"action":"subscribe","topic":"..."}` / `{"action":"unsubscribe","topic":"..."}`) for dynamic multi-topic subscriptions over a single connection. Outbound messages are wrapped in `{"topic":"...","data":{...}}` envelopes. Backward compatible with `?topic=` query parameter.

- **DLQ stats table filter**: `GET /v1/ops/dlq/stats` now accepts optional `?table=` query parameter to filter stats to a specific table.

- **TypeScript SDK `SharedWSManager`**: Single multiplexed WebSocket per client with ref-counted subscriptions, auto-reconnect with exponential backoff, and client-side NATS-style wildcard dispatch.

- **TypeScript SDK `LiveQuery`**: Stream-first backfill orchestrator — subscribes to the stream immediately, buffers events, fetches historical data, deduplicates by timestamp, then resumes live updates. Available via `queryBuilder.liveQuery(subscriber, opts?)`.

- **TypeScript SDK `FilteredStreamController`**: Streams created from query builders with active filters/columns now apply client-side filtering (eq, neq, gt, gte, lt, lte, in, like, not_like) and column projection.

- **TypeScript SDK `AbortSignal` support**: `stream({ signal })` option wires an `AbortSignal` to close the stream when aborted.

- **TypeScript SDK SSE connection counter**: Warns when opening more than 5 concurrent SSE connections (browser limit).

- **TypeScript SDK default query limit**: `QueryBuilder.DEFAULT_LIMIT = 1000` applied when no explicit limit is set, preventing unbounded result sets.

- **TypeScript SDK unit tests**: Comprehensive Vitest test suite for `@wavehouse/sdk` — `errors.test.ts`, `http.test.ts`, `query-builder.test.ts`, `table.test.ts`, `pipes.test.ts`, `namespaces.test.ts` (sql, schema, policy, DLQ, sys), `client.test.ts` (factory, namespace wiring, transport selection), `stream/controller.test.ts` (subscribe, unsubscribe, async iterator, ref counting).

- **TypeScript SDK codegen CLI**: `npm run codegen -- --url <url> --out <file>` introspects `/v1/ops/schema` and generates a TypeScript `Database` interface with ClickHouse-to-TypeScript type mapping (String, numeric, DateTime, Array, Map, Nullable, LowCardinality, etc.).

- **TypeScript SDK playground**: Three runnable scripts (`playground:public`, `playground:auth`, `playground:admin`) demonstrating unauthenticated queries/SSE, JWT auth/WebSocket streaming, and admin workflows (schema, policy, pipes, DLQ, raw SQL). Includes Docker Compose file and setup/seed script.

- **Generated artifacts moved to `tmp/`**: Coverage output now goes to `tmp/coverage/`, analysis artifacts (`size-map.*`, `graph.*`) to `tmp/analysis/`. Repo root is no longer cluttered with generated files. Updated Makefile, `.gitignore`, CI workflow, and all docs.

- **CGO scoped to build targets only**: Removed global `export CGO_ENABLED=0` from Makefile. `CGO_ENABLED=0` is now set per-command on `go build` invocations only. Test targets use the system default (`CGO_ENABLED=1`), which is required for `-race` on Linux CI runners. Tests continue to work on macOS (which has a pure-Go race detector) and now also work on Linux CI.

- **CI jobs parallelized**: `lint`, `test`, `build`, and `check` jobs now run fully in parallel. Previously `lint` waited for `check` and `integration` waited for `build`. Integration tests now gate only on `test` (unit tests must pass first).

- **GoReleaser config**: Added `ids` filter to `dockers_v2` (only linux builds go into Docker image) and OCI labels for image metadata.

- **`make vulncheck`**: Default output is now a summary (`-show=summary`). Use `V=1 make vulncheck` for full details.

- **`make clean`**: Simplified to `rm -rf bin/ tmp/ data/ dist/` — all generated files now live under `tmp/`.

- **CI workflow**: Refactored `.github/workflows/ci.yml` to use Makefile targets (`make mod-tidy-check`, `make fmt-check`, `make vulncheck`, `make coverage`, `make test-integration`). Pinned golangci-lint to v2.1.6.

- **`filterEventColumns()`**: Returns filtered copy instead of mutating shared event data (prevents cross-client data corruption during SSE/WS broadcast).

- **Hub bridge subscription**: `cmd/wavehouse-api/main.go` now fails startup on Subscribe error instead of suppressing it.

- **`make ci`**: Expanded to 7 steps: tidy + fmt + lint + vulncheck + build + unit tests + integration tests.

- **Pinned dev tools**: `gotestsum`, `gofumpt`, and `goimports` are tracked in `go.mod` via native `tool` directives (Go 1.24+). The Makefile uses `go run` — no manual tool installation needed. Eliminates `@latest` non-determinism.

- **Race detector enabled by default**: All test targets (`make test`, `make test-integration`, `make test-all`, `make ci`) now run with `-race`. Critical for catching data races in WaveHouse's concurrent NATS/cache/streaming code.

- **Integration tests bypass cache**: `-count=1` added to `make test-integration` to ensure tests always run against fresh Docker containers.

- **Makefile overhaul**: Added `make setup`, `make fmt` (gofumpt + goimports), `make ci` (full check: fmt + lint + tests). Replaced `make check` with `make ci`. Verbose output via `V=1 make test`. Colored output with `@`-prefixed commands.

- **CI workflow hardened**: Added formatting check (`gofumpt -l`) and module tidiness check (`go mod tidy` + `git diff --exit-code`). Integration tests now run with `-race -count=1`.
